\usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _{AB\tilde{H}}=\rho _{AB\tilde{H}}$$\end{document}ξABH~=ρABH~. Thus, the respective C.-J. states of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}\circ \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}N∘M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\overline{\mathcal {M}}\otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}})\circ \mathcal {D}$$\end{document}(M¯⊗N¯)∘D are equal, hence the two maps are equal.

To conclude the proof of Claim 2, we need to verify that the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}$$\end{document}D is TP as claimed. Note first that, by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M}}$$\end{document}M¯ and the TP property of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M (see Remark 2.4),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{tr}_A \circ \overline{\mathcal {M}} \circ \textrm{tr}_J = \textrm{tr}_{A H} \circ \mathcal {M} = \textrm{tr}_H = \textrm{tr}_{K I J}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trA∘M¯∘trJ=trAH∘M=trH=trKIJ.This implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_A \circ \overline{\mathcal {M}} = \textrm{tr}_{K I}$$\end{document}trA∘M¯=trKI, which means that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M}}$$\end{document}M¯ is TP. Similarly, one can see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {N}}$$\end{document}N¯ is TP. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}}$$\end{document}M¯⊗N¯, which goes from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K' \otimes K'' \otimes I \otimes J$$\end{document}K′⊗K′′⊗I⊗J to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \otimes B$$\end{document}A⊗B, is TP, too. Using this, then (4), and finally that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N} \circ \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}N∘M is TP, we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{tr}_{K' K'' I J} \circ \mathcal {D} = \textrm{tr}_{A B} \circ (\overline{\mathcal {M}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}}) \circ \mathcal {D} = \textrm{tr}_{A B} \circ \mathcal {N} \circ \mathcal {M} = \textrm{tr}_{K I J}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trK′K′′IJ∘D=trAB∘(M¯⊗N¯)∘D=trAB∘N∘M=trKIJ.This implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_{K' K''} \circ \mathcal {D} = \textrm{tr}_{K}$$\end{document}trK′K′′∘D=trK, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}$$\end{document}D is TP. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

With the proof of Claim 2, we have established (4). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 3.2

Theorem 3.1 follows from a more specialised version of the same where the maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N are assumed to be TP. To see this, note first that Lemma 2.11 immediately implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_A \circ \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}trA∘M is TP. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M must be TP anyway. It thus remains to show the following: For any map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N from H to B, there exists a TP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}'$$\end{document}N′ such that the correctness of Theorem 3.1 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}'$$\end{document}N′ implies the correctness of the theorem for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N.

Let thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N be a CP map that satisfies the assumptions of Theorem 3.1. From Remark 2.16 and the fact that H is finite-dimensional, we know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N can always be rescaled such that it is trace non-increasing. The rescaling does not alter Condition (i), Condition (iii), and (4). We can thus assume without loss of generality that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N is trace non-increasing. Let now \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}'$$\end{document}N′ be the TP extension of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N defined by Lemma 2.17, which maps from H to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B'$$\end{document}B′, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B' = B \oplus \textrm{span} \{|\perp \rangle \}$$\end{document}B′=B⊕span{|⊥⟩}. We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{tr}_{A}\circ \mathcal {N}' \circ \mathcal {M}&=\textrm{tr}_{A}\circ \,\mathcal {N}\circ \mathcal {M}+ \perp _{B'} \circ \big ( \textrm{tr}_A \circ \textrm{tr}_H \circ \mathcal {M}-\, \textrm{tr}_A \circ \textrm{tr}_B \circ \mathcal {N} \circ \mathcal {M} \big ) \\&=\mathcal {N}+ \perp _{B'} \circ \big (\textrm{tr}_H -\textrm{tr}_B \circ \mathcal {N} \big ) =\mathcal {N}'. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trA∘N′∘M=trA∘N∘M+⊥B′∘(trA∘trH∘M-trA∘trB∘N∘M)=N+⊥B′∘(trH-trB∘N)=N′.where the second equality holds because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N satisfies Condition (i), and because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M is TP. This shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}'$$\end{document}N′ also satisfies Condition (i). Furthermore, because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N satisfies Condition (iii) by assumption, it is independent of I, and hence Lemma 2.17 implies the same is true for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}'$$\end{document}N′. We have thus established that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}'$$\end{document}N′ is a CPTP map that meets all conditions of Theorem 3.1. The specialised version of Theorem 3.1 for TP maps now implies that there exists a CPTP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}$$\end{document}D such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {N}' \circ \mathcal {M} = \left( \overline{\mathcal {M}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}'}\right) \circ \mathcal {D}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}N′∘M=M¯⊗N′¯∘D.We can concatenate both sides with a projection map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi _B$$\end{document}ΠB onto the subspace B of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B'$$\end{document}B′. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N} = \Pi _B\circ \mathcal {N}'$$\end{document}N=ΠB∘N′ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {N}} = \Pi _B\circ \overline{\mathcal {N}'}$$\end{document}N¯=ΠB∘N′¯ we find that (4) and, hence, Theorem 3.1, holds for the general map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N.

Remark 3.3

The unitality requirement in Condition (ii) can be replaced by the weaker condition that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_A \circ \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}trA∘M is unital when restricted to the subsystem \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \otimes J$$\end{document}K⊗J of H, i.e.,21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{tr}_{A I} \circ \mathcal {M}(\rho _{I} \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{K J}) = {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{K J}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trAI∘M(ρI⊗idKJ)=idKJ.To see this, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M be such that it satisfies Conditions (i) and (iii), as well as (21). Furthermore, define a modified map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M'} :=\,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{I} \circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I \circ \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M′:=id¯I∘trI∘M. Because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N is independent of I, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M'}$$\end{document}M′ also satisfies Condition (i). Clearly, it also satisfies Condition (iii). And because of (21), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M'}$$\end{document}M′ also fulfils Condition (ii). We can thus apply the theorem to the modified map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M'}$$\end{document}M′, which implies that (4) holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}'$$\end{document}M′ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M'}}$$\end{document}M′¯. But \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M'}} = \overline{\mathcal {M}}$$\end{document}M′¯=M¯, which can be verified by using Remark 2.8:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \overline{\mathcal {M}'}&={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_H\circ \mathcal {M}'\circ \zeta _J\\&={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_H\circ \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{I}\circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I\circ \mathcal {M}\circ \zeta _J\\&={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_H\circ \mathcal {M}\circ \zeta _J\\&=\overline{\mathcal {M}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}M′¯=trH∘M′∘ζJ=trH∘id¯I∘trI∘M∘ζJ=trH∘M∘ζJ=M¯,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\zeta _J$$\end{document}ζJ is the map that creates a state \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\zeta _J$$\end{document}ζJ on J. Furthermore, again because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N is independent of I and can thus be written as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {N}}\circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I$$\end{document}N¯∘trI,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {N}\circ \mathcal {M}'&=\overline{\mathcal {N}}\circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I\circ \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{I}\circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I\circ \mathcal {M}\\&=\overline{\mathcal {N}}\circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I\circ \mathcal {M}\\&=\mathcal {N}\circ \mathcal {M}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}N∘M′=N¯∘trI∘id¯I∘trI∘M=N¯∘trI∘M=N∘M.Hence, (4) also holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M.

Remark 3.4

It is sufficient to prove Theorem 3.1 for finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces A and B, as this implies the general case where these systems have unbounded dimensions.

To see this, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N be CP maps for infinite-dimensional A and B that satisfy Conditions (i), (ii), and (iii) of Theorem 3.1. Furthermore, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\Pi _A^d)_{d \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(ΠAd)d∈N be a sequence of CP maps that project on d-dimensional nested subspaces of system A, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi _A^d \circ \Pi _A^{d'} = \Pi _A^{d}$$\end{document}ΠAd∘ΠA′=ΠAd for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d \le d'$$\end{document}d≤d′ such that, for all states \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _A$$\end{document}ρA,22\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{d \rightarrow \infty } \Pi ^d_A(\rho _A) = \rho _A \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limd→∞ΠAd(ρA)=ρASimilarly, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\Pi _B^d)_{d \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(ΠBd)d∈N a sequence of projection maps for the system B. We can then define sequences of CP maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {M}^d)_{d \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Md)d∈N and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {N}^d)_{d \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Nd)d∈N by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}^d&:=\bigl ( \Pi _A^d + \zeta _A \circ \textrm{tr}_A \circ (\mathcal {I}_A - \Pi _A^d)\bigr ) \circ \mathcal {M} \\ \mathcal {N}^d&:=\Pi _B^d \circ \mathcal {N}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Md:=(ΠAd+ζA∘trA∘(IA-ΠAd))∘MNd:=ΠBd∘N,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\zeta _A$$\end{document}ζA is an arbitrary state on A.

When considering the convergence of sequences of CP maps, we use the topology of their C.-J. representation as states, which in turn is induced by the trace distance.5 Thus, using (22),23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{d \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {M}^d&= \mathcal {M} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limd→∞Md=M24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{d \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {N}^d&= \mathcal {N} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limd→∞Nd=N.Furthermore, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}d∈N we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{tr}_A \circ \mathcal {M}^d = \textrm{tr}_A \circ \Pi _A^d \circ \mathcal {M} + \underbrace{\textrm{tr}_A(\zeta _A)}_{=1} (\textrm{tr}_A \circ \mathcal {M} - \textrm{tr}_A \circ \Pi _A^d \circ \mathcal {M}) = \textrm{tr}_A \circ \mathcal {M}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trA∘Md=trA∘ΠAd∘M+trA(ζA)⏟=1(trA∘M-trA∘ΠAd∘M)=trA∘M.Using this one can readily verify that each of the pairs of maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}^d$$\end{document}Md and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}^d$$\end{document}Nd satisfies Conditions (i), (ii), and (iii). Theorem 3.1 thus implies that there exists a CPTP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}^d$$\end{document}Dd such that25\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {N}^d \circ \mathcal {M}^d = \bigl ( \overline{\mathcal {M}^d} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}^d} \bigr ) \circ \mathcal {D}^d. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Nd∘Md=(Md¯⊗Nd¯)∘Dd.Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}^d$$\end{document}Dd is a CPTP map from K to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \otimes K$$\end{document}K⊗K, which is finite-dimensional. By the C.-J. isomorphism, the set of such maps is isomorphic to a closed subset of (normalised) states on a finite-dimensional space, which one may also purify. Furthermore, the set of pure states can be continuously embedded into a (real) Euclidean space, where it corresponds to a sphere of radius 1. Hence, the set of possible maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}^d$$\end{document}Dd is bounded and closed. We can thus employ the Bolzano-Weierstrass theorem, which tells us that there exists a subsequence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {D}^d)_{d \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Dd)d∈N that converges to a CPTP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}$$\end{document}D, i.e., there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(d_i)_{i \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(di)i∈N such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {D} = \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {D}^{d_i} \ . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}D=limi→∞Ddi.Note that the convergence of the sequences (23) and (24) also implies the convergence of the subsequences, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {M}^{d_i} = \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}limi→∞Mdi=M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim _{i\rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {N}^{d_i} = \mathcal {N}$$\end{document}limi→∞Ndi=N. Using this and (25) we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {N} \circ \mathcal {M}&= \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {N}^{d_i} \circ \mathcal {M}^{d_i} \\&= \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \ \bigl ( \overline{\mathcal {M}^{d_i}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}^{d_i}} \bigr ) \circ \mathcal {D}^{d_i} \\&= \bigl ( \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \overline{\mathcal {M}^{d_i}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}^{d_i}} \bigr ) \circ \bigl (\lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {D}^{d_i} \bigr ) \\&= \bigl ( \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \overline{\mathcal {M}^{d_i}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}^{d_i}} \bigr ) \circ \mathcal {D}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}N∘M=limi→∞Ndi∘Mdi=limi→∞(Mdi¯⊗Ndi¯)∘Ddi=(limi→∞Mdi¯⊗Ndi¯)∘(limi→∞Ddi)=(limi→∞Mdi¯⊗Ndi¯)∘D.Finally, Remark 2.8 implies that, for arbitrary states \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\zeta _I$$\end{document}ζI and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\zeta _J$$\end{document}ζJ, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M}^{d_i}} = \textrm{tr}_H \circ \mathcal {M}^{d_i} \circ \zeta _J$$\end{document}Mdi¯=trH∘Mdi∘ζJ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {N}^{d_i}} = \mathcal {N}^{d_i} \circ \zeta _I$$\end{document}Ndi¯=Ndi∘ζI. Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \overline{\mathcal {M}^{d_i}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}^{d_i}}\\&\quad = \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \textrm{tr}_{H} \circ \mathcal {M}^{d_i} \circ \zeta _J \otimes \mathcal {N}^{d_i} \circ \zeta _I \\&\quad = \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \bigl ( \Pi _A^{d_i} + \zeta _A \circ \textrm{tr}_A \circ (\mathcal {I}_A - \Pi _A^{d_i})\bigr ) \circ \textrm{tr}_H \circ \mathcal {M} \circ \zeta _J \otimes \Pi _B^{d_i} \circ \mathcal {N} \circ \zeta _I \\&\quad = \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \Bigl ( \Pi _A^{d_i} \otimes \Pi _B^{d_i} + \zeta _A \circ \textrm{tr}_A \circ (\mathcal {I}_A - \Pi _A^{d_i}) \otimes \Pi _B^{d_i} \Bigr ) \circ \bigl ( \textrm{tr}_H \circ \mathcal {M} \circ \zeta _J \otimes \mathcal {N} \circ \zeta _I \bigr ) \\&\quad = \underbrace{ \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \bigl (\Pi _A^{d_i} \otimes \Pi _B^{d_i} \bigr ) \circ \bigl ( \textrm{tr}_H \circ \mathcal {M} \circ \zeta _J \otimes \mathcal {N} \circ \zeta _I \bigr )}_{=\textrm{tr}_H \circ \mathcal {M} \circ \zeta _J \otimes \mathcal {N} \circ \zeta _I} \\&\qquad + \zeta _A \circ \textrm{tr}_A \circ \underbrace{ \lim _{i \rightarrow \infty } \bigl ( (\mathcal {I}_A - \Pi _A^{d_i}) \otimes \Pi _B^{d_i} \bigr ) \circ \bigl (\textrm{tr}_H \circ \mathcal {M} \circ \zeta _J \otimes \mathcal {N} \circ \zeta _I \bigr )}_{= 0}\\&\quad = \textrm{tr}_{H} \circ \mathcal {M} \circ \zeta _J \otimes \mathcal {N} \circ \zeta _I \\&\quad = \overline{\mathcal {M}} \otimes \overline{\mathcal {N}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limi→∞Mdi¯⊗Ndi¯=limi→∞trH∘Mdi∘ζJ⊗Ndi∘ζI=limi→∞(ΠAi+ζA∘trA∘(IA-ΠAi))∘trH∘M∘ζJ⊗ΠBi∘N∘ζI=limi→∞(ΠAi⊗ΠBi+ζA∘trA∘(IA-ΠAi)⊗ΠBi)∘(trH∘M∘ζJ⊗N∘ζI)=limi→∞(ΠAi⊗ΠBi)∘(trH∘M∘ζJ⊗N∘ζI)⏟=trH∘M∘ζJ⊗N∘ζI+ζA∘trA∘limi→∞((IA-ΠAi)⊗ΠBi)∘(trH∘M∘ζJ⊗N∘ζI)⏟=0=trH∘M∘ζJ⊗N∘ζI=M¯⊗N¯.Combining this with the equality above, we obtain (4).

As a preparation for our converse statement, Theorem 3.6, we first establish some general properties of the doubling map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}$$\end{document}D that comes out of Theorem 3.1.

Remark 3.5

The CPTP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}:K\rightarrow K'\otimes K''$$\end{document}D:K→K′⊗K′′ in Theorem 3.1 fulfils (i)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{\tilde{K}'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow \tilde{K}'\tilde{K}''}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow K' K''}=\mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' \tilde{K}''}$$\end{document}trK~′∘DK′′→K~′K~′′∘DK′′→K′K′′=DK→K′K~′′ and(ii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{K'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' K''}$$\end{document}trK′∘DK→K′K′′ is unital.(Note that all K’s are isomorphic spaces, but we use the notation above to distinguish them to keep track of where the different maps go.)

(i)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{\tilde{K}'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow \tilde{K}'\tilde{K}''}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow K' K''}=\mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' \tilde{K}''}$$\end{document}trK~′∘DK′′→K~′K~′′∘DK′′→K′K′′=DK→K′K~′′ and

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{\tilde{K}'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow \tilde{K}'\tilde{K}''}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow K' K''}=\mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' \tilde{K}''}$$\end{document}trK~′∘DK′′→K~′K~′′∘DK′′→K′K′′=DK→K′K~′′ and

(ii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{K'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' K''}$$\end{document}trK′∘DK→K′K′′ is unital.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{K'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' K''}$$\end{document}trK′∘DK→K′K′′ is unital.

To prove Property (i), we show how the maps on each side of the equality act on a general basis element \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(|a \rangle _{K_A^z}\otimes |b \rangle _{K_B^z})(\langle \bar{a} |_{K_A^{\bar{z}}} \otimes \langle \bar{b} |_{K_B^{\bar{z}}})$$\end{document}(|a⟩KAz⊗|b⟩KBz)(⟨a¯|KAz¯⊗⟨b¯|KBz¯) of the space of operators on K. For the right-hand side, applying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' \tilde{K}''}$$\end{document}DK→K′K~′′ as defined in (16) and (17) yields26where the colours indicate which parts combined yield a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-function on the respective labels. The left-hand side of (i) applied to the same bases element yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&{{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{\tilde{K}'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow \tilde{K}'\tilde{K}''}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow K' K''}\big ((|a \rangle _{K_A^z}\otimes |b \rangle _{K_B^z})(\langle \bar{a} |_{K_A^{\bar{z}}} \otimes \langle \bar{b} |_{K_B^{\bar{z}}})\big )\\&\hspace{20pt}={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{\tilde{K}'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow \tilde{K}'\tilde{K}''}\Big (\underbrace{|a \rangle \!\langle \bar{a} |_{K_A'^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{K_B'^z}}_{=:\rho _{K'}}\otimes \underbrace{\,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{K_A''^z}}_{=1/\dim (K_A^z)\sum _{\hat{a}}|\hat{a} \rangle \!\langle \hat{a} |_{K_A''^z}}\otimes |b \rangle \!\langle \bar{b} |_{K_B''^z}\Big )\cdot \delta _{z\bar{z}}\\&\hspace{20pt}={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{\tilde{K}'}\Big ({\textstyle \frac{1}{\dim (K_A^z)}} \sum _{\hat{a}}\mathcal {D}_{K''\rightarrow \tilde{K}'\tilde{K}''}\big (|\hat{a} \rangle \!\langle \hat{a} |_{K_A''^z}\otimes |b \rangle \!\langle \bar{b} |_{K_B''^z}\big )\Big )\otimes \rho _{K'}\cdot \delta _{z\bar{z}}\\&\hspace{20pt}={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{\tilde{K}'}\Big (\underbrace{{\textstyle \frac{1}{\dim (K_A^z)}}\sum _{\hat{a}}|\hat{a} \rangle \!\langle \hat{a} |_{\tilde{K}_A'^z}}_{=\,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{\tilde{K}_A'^z}}\otimes |b \rangle \!\langle \bar{b} |_{\tilde{K}_B''^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{\tilde{K}_B'^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{\tilde{K}_A''^z}\Big )\otimes \rho _{K'}\cdot \delta _{z\bar{z}}\\&\hspace{20pt}=|a \rangle \!\langle \bar{a} |_{K_A'^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{K_B'^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{\tilde{K}_A''^z}\otimes |b \rangle \!\langle \bar{b} |_{\tilde{K}_B''^z}\cdot \delta _{z\bar{z}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trK~′∘DK′′→K~′K~′′∘DK′′→K′K′′((|a⟩KAz⊗|b⟩KBz)(⟨a¯|KAz¯⊗⟨b¯|KBz¯))=trK~′∘DK′′→K~′K~′′(|a⟩⟨a¯|KA′z⊗id¯KB′z⏟=:ρK′⊗id¯KA′′z⏟=1/dim(KAz)∑a^|a^⟩⟨a^|KA′′z⊗|b⟩⟨b¯|KB′′z)·δzz¯=trK~′(1dim(KAz)∑a^DK′′→K~′K~′′(|a^⟩⟨a^|KA′′z⊗|b⟩⟨b¯|KB′′z))⊗ρK′·δzz¯=trK~′(1dim(KAz)∑a^|a^⟩⟨a^|K~A′z⏟=id¯K~A′z⊗|b⟩⟨b¯|K~B′′z⊗id¯K~B′z⊗id¯K~A′′z)⊗ρK′·δzz¯=|a⟩⟨a¯|KA′z⊗id¯KB′z⊗id¯K~A′′z⊗|b⟩⟨b¯|K~B′′z·δzz¯,which is equal to (26). Since this is true for any basis element of the space of operators on K, the maps are equal, proving (i). Property (ii) can be shown by a direct calculation, using that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{id}_K=\sum _{z,a,b}|a \rangle \!\langle a |_{K_A^z}\otimes |b \rangle \!\langle b |_{K_B^z}$$\end{document}idK=∑z,a,b|a⟩⟨a|KAz⊗|b⟩⟨b|KBz:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{K'}\circ \mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' K''}(\textrm{id}_K)&={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{K'}\Big (\sum _{a,b,z}\mathcal {D}_{K\rightarrow K' K''}\big (|a \rangle \!\langle a |_{K_A^z}\otimes |b \rangle \!\langle b |_{K_B^z}\big )\Big )\\&={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{K'}\Big (\sum _{a,b,z}|a \rangle \!\langle a |_{K_A'^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{K_B'^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{K_A''^z}\otimes |b \rangle \!\langle b |_{K_B''^z}\Big )\\&=\sum _z\underbrace{{{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_{K'}(\textrm{id}_{K_A'^z}\otimes \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{K_B'^z}}_{=\dim (K_A'^z)})\,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{K_A''^z}\otimes \textrm{id}_{K_B''^z}\\&=\sum _z\textrm{id}_{K_A''^z}\otimes \textrm{id}_{K_B''^z}\\&=\textrm{id}_{K''}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trK′∘DK→K′K′′(idK)=trK′(∑a,b,zDK→K′K′′(|a⟩⟨a|KAz⊗|b⟩⟨b|KBz))=trK′(∑a,b,z|a⟩⟨a|KA′z⊗id¯KB′z⊗id¯KA′′z⊗|b⟩⟨b|KB′′z)=∑ztrK′(idKA′z⊗id¯KB′z⏟=dim(KA′z))id¯KA′′z⊗idKB′′z=∑zidKA′′z⊗idKB′′z=idK′′.

We are now ready to state and prove the converse to Theorem 3.1. Identifying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {A}$$\end{document}A and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}$$\end{document}B with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M}}$$\end{document}M¯ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {N}}$$\end{document}N¯, respectively, it implies that the Conditions (i), (ii), and (iii) are necessary.

Theorem 3.6

(Converse statement to Theorem 3.1). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}:I\otimes K\otimes J\rightarrow A\otimes B$$\end{document}E:I⊗K⊗J→A⊗B be a CP map such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}=(\mathcal {A}\otimes \mathcal {B})\circ \mathcal {D}$$\end{document}E=(A⊗B)∘D for a CPTP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {A}:I\otimes K\rightarrow A$$\end{document}A:I⊗K→A and CP maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}:K\otimes J\rightarrow B$$\end{document}B:K⊗J→B and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}:K\rightarrow K\otimes K$$\end{document}D:K→K⊗K, where the latter fulfils Properties (i) and (ii) in Remark 3.5.6

Then there exist CP maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}:I\otimes K\otimes J\rightarrow A\otimes I\otimes K\otimes J$$\end{document}M:I⊗K⊗J→A⊗I⊗K⊗J and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}:I\otimes K\otimes J\rightarrow B$$\end{document}N:I⊗K⊗J→B such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}=\mathcal {N}\circ \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}E=N∘M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N fulfil Conditions (i), (ii), and (iii) in Theorem 3.1.

Proof

First, note that we can insert a map of the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I\circ \,\overline{\textrm{id}}_{I}$$\end{document}trI∘id¯I into \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {A}\otimes \mathcal {B})\circ \mathcal {D}$$\end{document}(A⊗B)∘D without changing the map. Remark 3.7

It has been shown in [9] that, if a map is unitary and satisfies a non-signalling condition analogous to Condition (iii) of Theorem 3.1, then this map has a structure similar to the right-hand side of (4). Note that the unitarity assumption is crucial for this result: the PR box [8, 10, 11] does not admit such a structure, although it satisfies the non-signalling condition. In contrast, Theorem 3.1 is valid for general (not necessarily unitary) CP maps, but instead requires the additional Conditions (i) and (ii) (which are also necessary; see Theorem 3.6).

Implications

Having established Theorem 3.1, we can give an answer to the question posed in the introduction, generalising Tsirelson’s result [15] to the “fully quantum” case. We state this answer as Corollary 4.1. Figure 5 illustrates the main assumption of the corollary—a commutation relation between the maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}X and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y described as Condition (i)—as well as the conclusion, which is that the concatenation of these two maps factorises; see (28).

The special case of Tsirelson’s result, which we discuss later as Corollary 4.2, refers to families of measurement operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{X_{i,\alpha }\}$$\end{document}{Xi,α} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{Y_{j,\beta }\}$$\end{document}{Yj,β} instead of CP maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}X and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y. Hence, Condition (i) of Corollary 4.1 can be understood as a quantum generalisation of Tsirelson’s assumption that the families of measurement operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{X_{i,\alpha }\}$$\end{document}{Xi,α} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{Y_{j,\beta }\}$$\end{document}{Yj,β} commute; see (29). Note that the measurement operators satisfy the property \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\smash {\sum _\alpha } X_{i,\alpha }=\textrm{id}_{K}$$\end{document}∑αXi,α=idK and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\smash {\sum _\beta } Y_{j,\beta }=\textrm{id}_{K}$$\end{document}∑βYj,β=idK. In Corollary 4.1, this property generalises to a unitality assumption, phrased as Condition (ii).7 This assumption is necessary; if we drop it without replacement, the statement is false, even when one restricts it to the purely classical case. This can be seen by choosing the maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}X and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trH∘Y∘X implements the PR box [8, 10, 11]. It is known that the PR box does not factorise, for this would violate the Tsirelson bound [13].8 We refer to Appendix B for more details.

Furthermore, the statement of Theorem 3.1 can be generalised to a family consisting of more than two maps which fulfil assumptions similar to Conditions (i)–(iii) in Theorem 3.1. We present this statement as Corollary 4.5.Fig. 5Visualisation of Corollary 4.1. Condition (i) holds if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document}X and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Y}$$\end{document}Y commute, in the sense that the two circuit diagrams on the left have the same input-output behaviour. Provided that the other conditions are also satisfied, the corollary implies that the circuit diagram shown to the right, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}X¯ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y¯ act on two separate copies of K, also has the same input-output behaviour. The diagram thus captures the idea that commuting maps factorise

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_A\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trA∘X or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_B\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trB∘Y is unital(iii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trH∘X is independent of J and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trH∘Y is independent of I.Then there exists a CPTP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}:K\rightarrow K\otimes K$$\end{document}D:K→K⊗K such that28\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}\circ {\mathcal {X}}=\big (\overline{\mathcal {X}}\otimes \overline{\mathcal \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {X}}\circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_J={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_H\circ \mathcal {X}$$\end{document}X¯∘trJ=trH∘X, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {Y}}\circ {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_I={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_H\circ \mathcal {Y}$$\end{document}Y¯∘trI=trH∘Y.

(i)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}\circ {\mathcal {X}}=\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {X}}\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trH∘Y∘X=trH∘X∘Y

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}\circ {\mathcal {X}}=\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {X}}\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trH∘Y∘X=trH∘X∘Y

(ii)either \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_A\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trA∘X or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_B\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trB∘Y is unital

either \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_A\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trA∘X or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_B\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trB∘Y is unital

(iii)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trH∘X is independent of J and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trH∘Y is independent of I.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trH∘X is independent of J and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trH∘Y is independent of I.

Proof

Without loss of generalisation, we assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_A\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}trA∘X is unital. If instead \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_B\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}trB∘Y is unital the same proof works by exchanging the roles of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}X and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y. To apply Theorem 3.1, we set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}:={\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}M:=X and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}:=\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}N:=trH∘Y, thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}\circ \mathcal {M}=\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}}\circ {\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}N∘M=trH∘Y∘X. Because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}X and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y commute under the trace over H, it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{tr}_A\circ \mathcal {N}\circ \mathcal {M}=\textrm{tr}_{AH}\circ {\mathcal {Y}}\circ {\mathcal {X}}=\textrm{tr}_{AH}\circ {\mathcal {X}}\circ {\mathcal {Y}} =\textrm{tr}_H\circ {\mathcal {Y}} =\mathcal {N}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trA∘N∘M=trAH∘Y∘X=trAH∘X∘Y=trH∘Y=N,hence Condition (i) in Theorem 3.1 is fulfilled. Conditions (ii) and (iii) are directly fulfilled by the definition of the maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {X}},{\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}X,Y. Hence, we can apply Theorem 3.1 and the statement of the corollary directly follows. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

If we specialise Corollary 4.1 to the case where the inputs I, J and the outputs A, B are classical, we retrieve the statement of [15] as described above. In fact, one may more generally consider a classical version of Theorem 3.1 instead of Corollary 4.1. This yields another generalisation of Tsirelson’s result, stated in Remark 4.4, which may be of independent interest.

Corollary 4.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{X_{i,\alpha }\}$$\end{document}{Xi,α} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{Y_{j,\beta }\}$$\end{document}{Yj,β} be finite families of positive operators on a finite-dimensional Hilbert space K such that29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {[}X_{i,\alpha },Y_{j,\beta }]=0 \quad \forall \, i,j,\alpha ,\beta , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[Xi,α,Yj,β]=0∀i,j,α,β,and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _\alpha X_{i,\alpha }=\textrm{id}_{K}$$\end{document}∑αXi,α=idK and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _\beta Y_{j,\beta }=\textrm{id}_{K}$$\end{document}∑βYj,β=idK for all i, j. Then there exists another finite-dimensional Hilbert space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{K}$$\end{document}K¯ with decomposition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{K}=K_A\otimes K_B$$\end{document}K¯=KA⊗KB and an isometry \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V: K\rightarrow \overline{K}$$\end{document}V:K→K¯ such that30\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} X_{i,\alpha }= V^* \bigl (A_{i,\alpha }\otimes \textrm{id}_{K_B}\bigr ) V ,\hspace{10pt} Y_{j,\beta }= V^* \bigl \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{i,\alpha }$$\end{document}Ai,α and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B_{j,\beta }$$\end{document}Bj,β are operators on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_A$$\end{document}KA and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_B$$\end{document}KB, respectively.

Proof

The first step in the proof is to apply Corollary 4.1 to the setting described above, therefore we have to identify the corresponding Hilbert spaces and maps and show that they fulfil the conditions of the theorem.
