Remark 4.3

It is actually not necessary in Corollary 4.2 to assume that for all i, j, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _\alpha X_{i,\alpha }=\textrm{id}_K$$\end{document}∑αXi,α=idK and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _\beta Y_{j,\beta }=\textrm{id}_K$$\end{document}∑βYj,β=idK. If this does not hold, we can scale the operators with a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma >0$$\end{document}γ>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{\alpha } X_{i, \alpha } \le \frac{1}{\gamma }\textrm{id}_K$$\end{document}∑αXi,α≤1γidK and add the operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X_{i,0}:=\textrm{id}_K-\gamma \sum _\alpha X_{i,\alpha }$$\end{document}Xi,0:=idK-γ∑αXi,α (and analogously for Y). This operator is also positive and commutes with all operators in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{Y_{j,\beta }\}$$\end{document}{Yj,β}.

Remark 4.4

As described above, Corollary 4.2 is obtained from Corollary 4.1 by treating I, J, A, and B as classical systems. We could apply the same procedure directly to Theorem 3.1. This allows us to derive a stronger version of Corollary 4.2, where (29) is replaced by the weaker condition that the two families of operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{X_{i,\alpha }\}$$\end{document}{Xi,α} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{Y_{j,\beta }\}$$\end{document}{Yj,β} satisfy40\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sum _\alpha \sqrt{X_{i, \alpha }} Y_{j, \beta } \sqrt{X_{i, \alpha }} = Y_{j, \beta } \quad \text { and } \forall \, i, j, \beta \ . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑αXi,αYj,βXi,α=Yj,βand∀i,j,β.Although the condition merely involves a sum over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α rather than a commutation relation for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α, it suffices to imply that the operators factorise as in (30).

To prove this, we define, analogously to (31),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}&: W_{H}\mapsto \sum _{i,\alpha }\left( |i \rangle \!\langle i |_{I}\otimes \sqrt{X_{i,\alpha }}\otimes \textrm{id}_{J}\right) W_{H}\left( |i \rangle \!\langle i |_{I}\otimes \sqrt{X_{i,\alpha }}\otimes \textrm{id}_{J}\right) \otimes |\alpha \rangle \!\langle \alpha |_A,\\ \mathcal {N}&: W_{H}\mapsto \sum _{j,\beta } \textrm{tr}_H \left( \textrm{id}_{I}\otimes \sqrt{Y_{j,\beta }}\otimes |j \rangle \!\langle j |_{J}\right) W_{H}\left( \textrm{id}_{I}\otimes \sqrt{Y_{j,\beta }}\otimes |j \rangle \!\langle j |_{J}\right) \otimes |\beta \rangle \!\langle \beta |_B. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}M:WH↦∑i,α|i⟩⟨i|I⊗Xi,α⊗idJWH|i⟩⟨i|I⊗Xi,α⊗idJ⊗|α⟩⟨α|A,N:WH↦∑j,βtrHidI⊗Yj,β⊗|j⟩⟨j|JWHidI⊗Yj,β⊗|j⟩⟨j|J⊗|β⟩⟨β|B.These CP maps manifestly satisfy Condition (iii) of Theorem 3.1. Furthermore, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M is identical to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {X}}$$\end{document}X as defined in the proof of Corollary 4.2, we already know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_A \circ \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}trA∘M is unital and trace-preserving, so Condition (ii) holds. Finally, Condition (i) is equivalent to the requirement that, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _K$$\end{document}ρK and all i, j,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sum _{\beta } {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_A \circ \mathcal {N} \circ \mathcal {M}(|i \rangle \!\langle i | \otimes \rho _K \otimes |j \rangle \langle j |) = \sum _{\beta } \mathcal {N}(|i \rangle \!\langle i | \otimes \rho _K \otimes |j \rangle \langle j |). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑βtrA∘N∘M(|i⟩⟨i|⊗ρK⊗|j⟩⟨j|)=∑βN(|i⟩⟨i|⊗ρK⊗|j⟩⟨j|).Inserting the explicit expressions for the maps, the requirement can be rewritten as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sum _{\alpha ,\beta } {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}\big (Y_{j, \beta } \sqrt{X_{i, \alpha }} \rho _K \sqrt{X_{i, \alpha }}\big )\otimes |\beta \rangle \!\langle \beta |_B = \sum _{\beta }{{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}\big (Y_{j, \beta } \rho _K\big )\otimes |\beta \rangle \!\langle \beta |_B \quad \forall \rho _K, i, j. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑α,βtr(Yj,βXi,αρKXi,α)⊗|β⟩⟨β|B=∑βtr(Yj,βρK)⊗|β⟩⟨β|B∀ρK,i,j.In particular, the equality must hold individually for each term of the sum over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta $$\end{document}β. It is thus equivalent to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sum _{\alpha } {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}\big (Y_{j, \beta } \sqrt{X_{i, \alpha }} \rho _K \sqrt{X_{i, \alpha }}\big ) = {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}\big (Y_{j, \beta } \rho _K\big ) \quad \forall \rho _K, i, j,\beta , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑αtr(Yj,βXi,αρKXi,α)=tr(Yj,βρK)∀ρK,i,j,β,which in turn is equivalent to (40).

Corollary 4.5

Starting from (44) and using this induction step repeatedly, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_K\circ \mathcal {M}_s \circ \dots \circ \mathcal {M}_1=\left( \overline{\mathcal {M}}_1\otimes \dots \otimes \overline{\mathcal {M}}_s\right) \circ \mathcal {D}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trK∘Ms∘⋯∘M1=M¯1⊗⋯⊗M¯s∘D,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathcal {M}}_i={{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}_K\circ \mathcal {M}_i$$\end{document}M¯i=trK∘Mi and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {D}=\mathcal {D}^{(s-1)}\circ \dots \circ \mathcal {D}^{(1)}$$\end{document}D=D(s-1)∘⋯∘D(1) (see Fig. 6). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 4.6

In the same way as Corollary 4.1 replaces Condition (i) of Theorem 3.1 by a commutation condition, one may replace assumption (41) of Corollary 4.5 on the maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_1, \ldots , \mathcal {M}_s$$\end{document}M1,…,Ms by a commutation assumption, namely that changing the order of the maps in the concatenation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{tr}_K \circ \mathcal {M}_s \circ \cdots \circ \mathcal {M}_1$$\end{document}trK∘Ms∘⋯∘M1 does not have an effect. This results in a statement similar to Corollary 4.1: If the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \ge 2$$\end{document}s≥2 maps on the left-hand side of (42) commute and satisfy the unitality condition, then they factorise as on the right-hand side of (42).
